family meals, Edmond agreed to see a mental health professional at the urging of his parents and HR.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since the shooting I feel like I’m constantly on guard, checking every room and jumpy about anything happening around me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Since the shooting my brain just feels fogged out—I'll be in the middle of a sales call or even a simple email and lose my train of thought completely, staring at the screen because I can't decide on basic things like what to write or which client to call next.\n- S071 (Trauma_Related_Amnesia): I can picture being outside the bar and then suddenly I'm behind the parked car with glass everywhere, but the actual moment the guy pulled the gun and started firing is just a black hole in my head, like that whole chunk of time got erased even though I remember everything right before and after it way too clearly.\n- S076 (Inability_Positive_Emotions): Even when my team closed a big deal this week and everyone was high‑fiving and my dad tried to celebrate me sticking with work, I just felt flat and weirdly disconnected, like I was watching someone else's life and couldn't feel any real happiness or warmth about it at all.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Now, wherever I go—client dinners, the office, even my parents’ house—I automatically clock every exit, keep an eye on who’s coming in and out, and sit with my back to the wall because my body won't let me relax for a second, like something bad could explode out of nowhere again.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If someone drops a stapler at work or a car backfires outside, I jump out of my skin, my heart slams in my chest, and I’m left shaking and on edge for a long time after everyone else has already laughed it off and moved on.\n- S082 (Depersonalization_Derealization): A lot of the time lately it feels like I’m moving through a movie of my life instead of actually living it—people’s voices sound far away, colors look kind of washed out, and I catch myself thinking, ‘This can’t be real,’ like I’m floating just outside my own body watching myself go through the motions.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I skipped that big evening client event at the bar, my sales numbers have started to slide, and even my family meals feel tense because I’m so on edge that people keep asking what’s wrong with me.\n- duration: These problems have been going on for about ten days now, basically since the night of the shooting.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: “Ever since the shooting I feel like I’m constantly on guard, checking every room and jumpy about anything happening around me.”\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. This is the very first time I've experienced anything like this, and it all started exactly ten days ago right after the shooting.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you been having trouble falling or staying asleep, or have you noticed yourself feeling unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any trouble sleeping, and I wouldn't say I've been feeling unusually angry or irritable. But my heart definitely slams in my chest and I start shaking when I'm startled by sudden loud noises, like a car backfiring or a dropped stapler at the office.\n\nDoctor: When you thought about attending that client event, or whenever you are confronted with reminders of the shooting, do you experience intense feelings of anxiety, fear, or distress?\n\nPatient: Yes, I felt an overwhelming sense of anxiety and dread just thinking about it. This is the very first time I've experienced anything like this, and it all started exactly ten days ago right after the shooting.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you been having trouble falling or staying asleep, or have you noticed yourself feeling unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any trouble sleeping, and I wouldn't say I've been feeling unusually angry or irritable. It's more just that constant feeling of being on guard and jumpy during the day.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't have clear visual memories, do you ever suddenly feel or act as if the shooting is actually happening all over again in that very moment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm actually back in that exact moment reliving it. While I'm constantly terrified and on guard that something like that might happen again, I don't lose touch with reality or feel like the shooting is happening all over again in the present.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on your work or other daily tasks since the shooting?"

It's more that I'm just constantly tense and on edge during the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself actively avoiding the area where the shooting happened, or trying to avoid thinking or talking about it?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I'm completely avoiding crowded bars and popular nightlife spots now, even though that's how I usually network for my job. I actually had to skip a major evening client event last week because I just couldn't bring myself to face going back into that kind of environment.\n\nDoctor: Even though you haven't had nightmares or flashbacks, do you find sudden, unwanted thoughts or images of the shooting popping into your mind during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get sudden thoughts or images of it popping up during the day. Honestly, the actual moment of the shooting feels more like a blank space or a fog in my head rather than something I keep picturing.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you felt emotionally numb, or detached and cut off from your family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel completely numb and disconnected lately. Even when my team closed a big deal this week and my dad tried to celebrate with me, I just felt totally flat and unable to feel any real happiness or warmth. It honestly feels like I'm just watching someone else's life instead of living my own.\n\nDoctor: When you are confronted with things that remind you of the shooting, do you experience physical reactions like a racing heart, sweating, or trouble breathing?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed those specific physical reactions just from reminders, mainly because I've been actively avoiding bars and crowded places altogether. But my heart definitely slams in my chest and I start shaking when I'm startled by sudden loud noises, like a car backfiring or a dropped stapler at the office.\n\nDoctor: When you thought about attending that client event, or whenever you are confronted with reminders of the shooting, do you experience intense feelings of anxiety, fear, or distress?\n\nPatient: Yes, I felt an overwhelming sense of anxiety and dread just thinking about it. I had to skip that client event because I simply couldn't face going back to a crowded bar, and even the thought of being in that kind of environment makes me feel incredibly tense and overwhelmed with fear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these kinds of symptoms for more than a month in the past, or is this the first time you have felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like this before in my life. This is the very first time I've experienced anything like this, and it all started exactly ten days ago right after the shooting.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you been having trouble falling or staying asleep, or have you noticed yourself feeling unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any trouble sleeping, and I wouldn't say I've been feeling unusually angry or irritable. It's more just that constant feeling of being on guard and jumpy during the day.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't have clear visual memories, do you ever suddenly feel or act as if the shooting is actually happening all over again in that very moment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't ever feel like I'm actually back in that exact moment reliving it. While I'm constantly terrified and on guard that something like that might happen again, I don't lose touch with reality or feel like the shooting is happening all over again in the present.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on your work or other daily tasks since the shooting?\n\nPatient: Yes, my brain has felt completely fogged out since the shooting. I'll be in the middle of a sales call or a simple email and completely lose my train of thought. I just end up staring at my screen because I can't seem to make basic decisions on what to do next.\n\nDoctor: Since the shooting, have you felt a constant sense of worry, or a feeling that you are unable to cope or plan for the future?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel this constant, exhausting tension because my body is always on edge, waiting for something terrible to happen again. It really feels like I can't cope with daily life anymore—I'm skipping social events, failing to handle my normal responsibilities, and I can't even make simple decisions at work like who I need to call next."

The patient explicitly denies any intrusive memories, flashbacks, or nightmares (reliving symptoms), which are required for a diagnosis of F43.1 (Post-traumatic stress disorder). Therefore, F43.1 is ruled out. The clinical presentation of anxiety, hyperarousal, avoidance, emotional numbing, and significant impairment in social and occupational functioning following a severe stressor fits the criteria for F43.2 (Adjustment disorder). Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.
